## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear argument that runs from framing (slides 2-10), through model building and deployment, to a closing 'no-one knows' stance, with section dividers and consistent takeaway titles such as 'FOMO drives a capex surge' (13). Charts are clean, sourced, and use red sparingly to point at the key series, and the three-circle device on slides 8 and 12 repeats a simple structure the audience learns quickly. Weaknesses are many near-empty quote slides and circle slides, small chart labels and tiny footers, a left margin that leaves dead space (6, 13), and a few weak pages such as 22, 38 and 41, where the image or screenshot adds little.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the action-style titles with a one-line subtitle, the restrained red-on-grey palette that highlights one series per chart, and the sourced, uncluttered charts like 13 and 14.

**Most useful repair:** Enlarge axis labels, legends and in-chart text on the chart slides, and make the screenshots on 38 and 41 legible or crop them to the one point that matters.

**What would change the score:** I would merge or cut some of the sparse quote and circle slides (4, 5, 9, 25, 50) and add a single annotation per chart to state the takeaway directly.

_Confidence high. 59 of 59 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide and a sequence of quotations about AI, framing the question of whether generative AI is just another platform shift or something larger. It then moves through named sections: model building, where the author examines capital spending, falling model prices, open source and the lack of obvious moats; deployment, where he argues that LLMs make software probabilistic and that enterprises face slow, uncertain adoption; and a closing section on how past platform shifts and new technologies have been absorbed, disrupted or redefined. Later slides cover startups unbundling incumbents, where advertising money flows, and how industries change over time. The deck ends with a short biography slide and a thank-you slide. Slides worth studying include the platform-shift timeline on slide 6, the simple three-circle framing on slide 8, the Microsoft margin chart on slide 14, and the large-number chart on slide 13, where data and short takeaway headlines carry the argument.

## Questions about this deck

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about price changes for AI models?**

Slide 18 is titled "Ongoing price collapse" and quotes "An LLM is the most expensive and fastest-depreciating asset in tech history." It shows price per MTOK ($) for model equivalence on a log scale from Dec 2022 to Dec 2024, sourced from the Stanford AI Index.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about the surge in capital spending on AI?**

Slide 13 is titled "FOMO drives a capex surge" and says "If this is the centre of tech for the next decade, you don't want to miss it." Its chart of capex ($bn) covers Meta, Alphabet, AWS and Microsoft from 2010 to 2025e.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about how enterprises are adopting generative AI?**

Slide 44 says "A quarter of CIOs have launched something - but 40% don't plan anything until at least 2026." Slide 43 adds that enterprise software takes time and comes with early disappointments.

**How is the Benedict Evans deck structured into sections?**

Slide 11 marks the "Model building" section and slide 26 marks the "Deployment" section. Slide 35 opens the closing discussion with the question "What's the standard pattern?" and lists the responses as absorb, innovate and disrupt.

Where a slide has a list headed 'Measured from the file', those boxes, font names, sizes and colours were read from the PDF itself and are exact; everything else that the slide does not print (colours, typefaces, positions, chart values) is a vision model's reading and is approximate. ### Slide 6

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A single curved arrow line per platform, with the generative AI line drawn dotted in the accent colour, makes the argument visible at a glance.
- Review: The deck has a clear argument that runs from framing (slides 2-10), through model building and deployment, to a closing 'no-one knows' stance, with section dividers and consistent takeaway titles such as 'FOMO drives a capex surge' (13).

**Text**

So, the next platform shift?
Every 10-15 years, a platform shift reshapes the tech industry
Mainframes
PCs
Web
Smartphones
Generative AI
Benedict Evans –– May 2025
6

**Layout**

Full-width title block at top with the title left-aligned at about x 20.5% and a subtitle directly beneath it; below sits a white band holding five equal-width vertical columns separated by thin light-grey gutters, each with a curved arrow rising left to right and a label centred at the bottom of the column. The footer line sits right-aligned near the bottom with the author, date and page number, on a grey field.

**Visuals**

Five curved connector arrows, each drawn as a smooth S-curve rising from the lower-left of one column into the next, with the arrowhead at the right end. The first four arrows (Mainframes, PCs, Web, Smartphones) are solid mid-grey #808080 strokes of about 0.3% slide height with grey filled triangular arrowheads. The fifth arrow, leaving the Generative AI column from the left and rising to the upper right, is a red #EB483F dotted stroke with a red filled triangular arrowhead, marking the next shift. Each arrow starts at the bottom of the previous column's label area and climbs upward: Mainframes arrow starts near y 76% and ends near y 56%; PCs arrow starts near y 62% and ends near y 48%; Web arrow starts near y 56% and ends near y 42%; Smartphones arrow starts near y 50% and ends near y 36%; Generative AI arrow starts near y 42% and ends near y 27%. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer, with a white #FFFFFF central band. Title and subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, title colour #545454 at about 5.6% of slide height (cap height about 3.9%), subtitle in the same serif colour #545454 at about 2.6%. Column labels in MessinaModern-Book serif at about 3.0% in #545454, except Generative AI in #EB483F. Footer in MessinaSans, Benedict Evans in Bold and the date and page number in Book, all at about 1.85% in #A6A6A6. Column separators are light grey strips about 0.5% wide, the same grey as the background. Arrows are thin strokes with no shadows and no rounded corners on the columns.

### Slide 7

**Review notes**

- Review: The deck has a clear argument that runs from framing (slides 2-10), through model building and deployment, to a closing 'no-one knows' stance, with section dividers and consistent takeaway titles such as 'FOMO drives a capex surge' (13).

**Text**

So, the next platform shift? Or more? / But if these models really do keep scaling, the change could be a lot bigger / ? / Mainframes / PCs / Web / Smartphones / Generative AI? / Benedict Evans / –– May 2025 / 7

**Layout**

Centred serif title at the top, with a left-aligned serif subtitle beneath it, on a light grey band spanning the top fifth of the slide. A white panel fills the middle band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of the height and is divided into five vertical columns by thin grey gaps; the first column at the left is empty and the other four each hold a grey curved arrow and a centred label, with the last column labelled in red. A grey footer band sits below the panel, carrying a right-aligned attribution line and page number.

**Visuals**

Five grey curved arrows and two red dotted arrows, drawn as a diagram of platform shifts. Grey solid arrows, each an S-shaped curve rising left to right with a filled triangular arrowhead, run from the bottom-left of the Mainframes column through the PCs, Web and Smartphones columns, each ending at the left edge of the next column; the Smartphones arrow ends at the left edge of the Generative AI column. From that point a red dotted line splits into two: one dotted curve rises steeply up and off the top of the slide with a filled red arrowhead at about 2% from the top; a second dotted curve rises gently to the right and ends in a filled red arrowhead at the right side of the Generative AI column. A red question mark sits in the wedge between the two red dotted arrows. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel across the middle band. Flat design with no gradients, shadows or rounded corners. Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the top band and footer; the middle panel is white #FFFFFF. Title and subtitle text #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif face. Column labels #545454 in MessinaModern-Book, with the final label and the question mark in red accent #EB483F. Arrows are grey #808080 solid strokes of about 0.3% of slide height with filled triangular heads; the red arrows are #EB483F dotted strokes of the same weight. Title text is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle and labels about 3.0% of slide height, footer about 1.9%. Footer text is sans-serif MessinaSans in light grey #A6A6A6, with 'Benedict Evans' in bold and the date in book weight.

### Slide 8

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Three circles with the central one highlighted in red set up the central question in one image with almost no text.
- Review: The deck has a clear argument that runs from framing (slides 2-10), through model building and deployment, to a closing 'no-one knows' stance, with section dividers and consistent takeaway titles such as 'FOMO drives a capex surge' (13).
- Review: Charts are clean, sourced, and use red sparingly to point at the key series, and the three-circle device on slides 8 and 12 repeats a simple structure the audience learns quickly.

**Text**

What kind of a change is ‘generative AI’? / Is this Everything, or just the New Thing? / ‘Just’ more software / Thousands of models! / How far will this keep getting better? / One world computer controls everything! / Benedict Evans –– May 2025 / 8

**Layout**

Single-row composition of three equal circles centred horizontally between two small arrow triangles, on a plain light-grey field. The title sits top-left at about 20.5% from the left edge, with a smaller subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned on a shared left edge. The footer runs along the bottom right with the author name, date and page number on one baseline, and there is no footer rule.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles of equal size arranged left to right in a row: a dark grey circle at left, a red circle in the centre, and a dark grey circle at right. Between the circles are two small grey triangles, the left one pointing left between the left and centre circles and the right one pointing right between the centre and right circles. The circles are flat with no outline or shadow. No photographs, charts or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat light grey background (#F1F1F1). Title and subtitle in a serif face (Messina Modern Regular) in grey #545454. Circle text in Messina Modern Book, white #FFFFFF, centred. Footer in Messina Sans, light grey #A6A6A6, with the author name in bold. Title is about 5.6% of slide height and body text about 3.3%. Circles are flat fills #535353 and #EB483F with no outlines, shadows or gradients. Arrow triangles are flat grey #A6A6A6 with no outline.

### Slide 9

**Review notes**

- Review: The deck has a clear argument that runs from framing (slides 2-10), through model building and deployment, to a closing 'no-one knows' stance, with section dividers and consistent takeaway titles such as 'FOMO drives a capex surge' (13).

**Text**

“In from three to eight / years we will have a / machine with the general / intelligence of an average / human being” / Marvin Minsky, 1970 / “AI is whatever machines / can’t do yet” / Larry Tesler, 1970 / Benedict Evans / –– May 2025 / 9

**Layout**

Two equal columns split at the vertical centre: a left grey panel (x 0–50) and a right white panel (x 50–100). Each column holds a quotation block vertically centred around the middle of the slide, left-aligned at about 5.4% (left column) and 55.3% (right column) from the left edge. A small footer line sits at about y 91% across the right column, with the page number at the far right near the margin.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background is split into two flat panels: left panel light grey #F2F2F2, right panel white #FFFFFF. All quotation text is set in red #EB483F in a book-weight serif (MessinaModern-Book, a modern-style serif with high contrast and vertical stress), regular weight, sentence case, no italics, at about 5.6% of slide height with generous line spacing (about 6.7% of slide height between baselines). Quotation marks are curly. Footer text is grey #A6A6A6 in a sans-serif (MessinaSans), about 1.9% of slide height: 'Benedict Evans' in Bold, followed by '–– May 2025' and page number '9' in Book weight. No shapes, lines or rules; square corners; no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 10

**Review notes**

- Review: The deck has a clear argument that runs from framing (slides 2-10), through model building and deployment, to a closing 'no-one knows' stance, with section dividers and consistent takeaway titles such as 'FOMO drives a capex surge' (13).

**Text**

What’s going on right now? / Two kinds of conversation - this is Something, but it might be Everything / Model Building + Software Deployment / “This will transform humanity!” / Benedict Evans –– May 2025 / 10

**Layout**

Title sits top-left at x 20.5%, with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned on a shared left edge. Two equal circles are centred on a horizontal midline (about y 34% to 73%), with a dotted vertical divider centred between them at x about 53%. The footer runs along the bottom at y 91% with the author name, date and a right-side page number at x 94.5%. No grid lines or boxes; the layout is open with generous white space.

**Visuals**

Two solid filled circles of equal size (22.1% wide by 39.3% tall, so roughly 0.56 aspect-corrected circles) and one vertical dotted line. Left circle: solid red #EB483F, containing white serif text 'Model / Building / + / Software / Deployment' in centred stacked lines. Right circle: solid dark grey #535353, containing white serif text '“This will / transform / humanity!”' in centred stacked lines. Dotted divider: about 0.3% wide, dotted line in red #EB483F, running vertically from about y 31.6% to y 78% at x about 53%, midway between the circles. No photographs, icons or logos. No chart or table.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 across the full slide. Flat, minimal look on a light grey background. Title is in MessinaModern-Regular serif, colour #545454, about 5.56% of slide height. Subtitle is MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, about 2.59% of slide height. Text inside circles is MessinaModern-Book serif, white #FFFFFF, about 3.33% of slide height. Accent colour is red #EB483F (left circle and dotted line); secondary is charcoal grey #535353 (right circle). Footer is MessinaSans, author name Bold and date/page number Book, colour #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of slide height. Shapes are plain circles with no outline, no shadow, no gradient and no corner radius.

### Slide 12

**Review notes**

- Review: Charts are clean, sourced, and use red sparingly to point at the key series, and the three-circle device on slides 8 and 12 repeats a simple structure the audience learns quickly.

**Text**

Two years of the model wars / Lots of investment, lots of progress, no apparent defensibility / Much more capital / Pricing collapse / Lots of new stuff - agents, multimodal, ‘reasoning’, MCP etc / No obvious moats / Open Source / Benedict Evans –– May 2025 / 12

**Layout**

Left-aligned title and subtitle sit in the upper-left at about 20.5% from the left edge, with the left fifth of the slide left empty. Three equal circles of 22.1% width are spaced evenly in a horizontal row across the middle band, vertically centred near y 33.9% to 73.2%. The footer line sits bottom-right at y 91.1%, with the page number at the far right edge (about 94.5% to 95.5%).

**Visuals**

Three filled circles of equal size in a row. Circle 1 at x 19.5%, y 33.9%, 22.1% by 39.3%, solid #535353 dark grey, containing the white text 'Much more capital' and 'Pricing collapse'. Circle 2 at x 46.8%, same size and y, solid #535353, containing 'Lots of new stuff - agents, multimodal, ‘reasoning’, MCP etc'. Circle 3 at x 74.1%, same size and y, solid #EB483F red, containing 'No obvious moats' and 'Open Source'. All circles are flat with no outline, no shadow and no gradient. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat colour #F1F1F1 light grey. Flat light grey background #F1F1F1 with no pattern. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, regular weight, colour #545454, about 5.56% of slide height. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, about 2.59% of slide height. Circle text in MessinaModern-Book serif, white #FFFFFF, about 3.33% of slide height, centred, line spacing about 4.5% between wrapped lines and a larger gap between paragraphs. Footer and page number in MessinaSans sans-serif, #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of slide height; 'Benedict Evans' bold, the rest book weight. Accent colour is red #EB483F, used on the third circle only. Circles are perfect circles with no corner radius, shadow or gradient.

### Slide 13

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A stacked capex chart with a plain headline stating the takeaway turns a dense dataset into one clear point.

**Text**

FOMO drives a capex surge
If this is the centre of tech for the next decade, you don’t want to miss it

13

**Layout**

Full-width grey header band (top 20.5%) holds the left-aligned serif title at about 5.6% from top and a subtitle beneath it, both starting at x 20.5%. A white content band fills the middle (y 20.5% to 86.1%) with the chart spanning roughly x 4.8% to 95% on a left-aligned legend column and a y-axis label column at x 17.8%, year labels on the bottom axis, and a grey footer band below with the source lines at x 20.5%, the author credit, date and slide number on the right at x 74.9% to 95.5%.

**Visuals**

Stacked vertical bar chart titled Capex ($bn), with the y-axis running from 0 to 350 in steps of 50 and labelled in $bn, and the x-axis listing calendar years 2010 to 2024 plus 2025e (estimate). Four series stacked bottom to top: Microsoft (red #EE4B3E), AWS* (salmon #F4A29C), Alphabet (light pink #F2D5D1), Meta (dark grey #545454). No values are printed on the bars; approximate totals read from the chart: 2010 about 7; 2011 about 8; 2012 about 9; 2013 about 17; 2014 about 21; 2015 about 25; 2016 about 36; 2017 about 37; 2018 about 62; 2019 about 64; 2020 about 62; 2021 about 95; 2022 about 122; 2023 about 132; 2024 about 217; 2025e about 315. Approximate Microsoft segments: 2016 about 10, 2018 about 14, 2020 about 20, 2021 about 27, 2022 about 28, 2023 about 40, 2024 about 76, 2025e about 95. Approximate AWS segments: 2021 about 24, 2022 about 31, 2023 about 32, 2024 about 48, 2025e about 75. Approximate Alphabet segments: 2021 about 25, 2022 about 32, 2023 about 28, 2024 about 53, 2025e about 75. Approximate Meta segments: 2021 about 18, 2022 about 31, 2023 about 27, 2024 about 40, 2025e about 70. Legend at top left of chart in order Meta, Alphabet, AWS*, Microsoft, each with a small filled square. Dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at every 50 units. No highlighted points. The 2025e bar is the final bar and is labelled with the e suffix. Bars are boxed in the plot area with thin white separators between segments.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Two-tone neutral look: light grey header and footer bands framing a white middle band. Title colour dark grey #545454 in a high-contrast Messina Modern-style serif, regular weight, large. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, smaller, colour #545454. Chart and footer text in a humanist sans-serif (Messina Sans style), regular weight, colour #545454; chart caption in semibold. Author name bold grey #A6A6A6, date and slide number in book weight grey #A6A6A6. Accents are a red and pink family: Microsoft #EE4B3E, AWS #F4A29C, Alphabet #F2D5D1, Meta #545454. Title text about 5.6% of slide height, body text about 1.9% of slide height. Shapes are square-cornered with no shadows or gradients; gridlines are thin dotted grey lines.

### Slide 14

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A single line chart annotated with a short ironic caption shows how a data view can carry a narrative twist.

**Text**

Microsoft’s margin shift / Remember when Microsoft’s business was selling $1 CDs for $250 each? / Microsoft capex/sales (TTM) / 40% / 30% / 20% / 10% / 0% / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / Jun 2025 / guidance / Source: Microsoft. Includes capital leases / Benedict Evans / –– May 2025 / 14

**Layout**

Two horizontal bands frame the slide: a light grey header band across the top (about 0 to 20.5% of height) and a light grey footer band below the white chart area (about 86 to 100%). The title sits at top-left at x 20.5%, with the subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned to the same edge. The chart block starts at x 20.6% with its heading above the plot; the y-axis labels sit left of the plot at x about 17.5 to 20%, and the plot runs to about x 95%. The source line is left-aligned at x 20.5% in the footer; the author, date and slide number sit right-hand in the footer, ending near x 96%.

**Visuals**

One line chart, titled 'Microsoft capex/sales (TTM)'. Y-axis: percentage, 0% to 40%, gridlines and labels at 0%, 10%, 20%, 30% and 40%. X-axis: years 2000 to Jun 2025 guidance, labelled at 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020 and 'Jun 2025 guidance' at the right end. Single series, a smooth red line about 3 pt wide, with no printed data values. Estimated values, marked 'about': 2000 about 3.8%; 2001 about 4.4%; 2002 about 2.7%; 2005 about 2.0% (the low point); 2008 about 5.2%; 2009 about 5.5%; 2010 about 3.2%; 2013 about 5.5%; 2015 about 6.3%; 2016 about 10.5%; 2019 about 13.4%; 2020 about 13.3%; 2023 about 14.8%; the line rises steeply to about 31.5% at Jun 2025 guidance. No legend. No highlighted points; the final point at the right edge is the endpoint of the line. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey lines. The horizontal axis at 0% is a solid thin grey rule.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from about y 20.5% to 86%. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands with a white (#FFFFFF) central panel. Title in a modern serif (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a transitional serif) in dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height, regular weight. Subtitle in the same serif, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart heading and all chart labels, source line and footer text in a sans-serif (MessinaSans, close to a grotesque sans), #545454 for chart text, about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height; chart heading in SemiBold. Footer author name in Bold #A6A6A6, date in Book #A6A6A6, slide number in Book #A6A6A6. Accent colour: red line about #E8483B. Gridlines light grey dotted. No shadows, gradients or corner radii.

### Slide 15

**Text**

The Nvidia moat / Nvidia tries to build a new Sun with custom compute and a software ecosystem. (This is also a TSMC chart) / Quarterly revenue ($bn) / Intel / Nvidia / 50 / 40 / 30 / 20 / 10 / 0 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / 2024 / Source: Companies / Benedict Evans / –– May 2025 / 15

**Layout**

A full-width grey header band holds the title and subtitle, both left-aligned on a shared left edge at about 20.5% of slide width. Below it, a white panel fills the middle of the slide from about 20.5% to 86% of height, containing a single line chart whose plot area runs from about 18% to 95% of width, with a legend at the far left (about 4.8%) outside the chart area. A grey footer band at the bottom holds the source line at left, the author, date and slide number at right, with the slide number at about 94.5% of width.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Quarterly revenue ($bn)'. Y-axis: revenue in $bn, range 0 to 50, labelled ticks at 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, with faint dotted horizontal gridlines at each tick. X-axis: calendar years from 2000 to about 2025, labelled ticks at 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020, 2024, with a thin solid grey baseline at 0. Two series drawn as smoothed lines about 0.4% of slide height thick. Intel (dark grey #545454 line): about 8 in 2000, dipping to about 6.5 in 2001-2003, about 10 in 2008, about 7 in 2009, about 10.5 in 2010, about 13 in 2011, about 13.5 in 2013, about 15 in 2015, peaking about 20 around 2020 and 2022, about 15 in 2023, and about 14 at the end. Nvidia (red #E8453C line): about 0.2 from 2000 to 2007, about 1 in 2008-2013, about 2 in 2016, about 3 in 2018, about 8 in 2022, dropping to about 6 in 2023, then rising steeply to about 39 at the right edge after 2024. Legend at left: short dark grey line segment beside 'Intel' and short red line segment beside 'Nvidia', stacked vertically. No highlighted points or annotations. No values are printed on the chart itself, so all values are estimates.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF panel from about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Light grey header and footer bands with a white middle panel. Title in a MessinaModern-Regular serif, dark grey #545454, large. Subtitle in the same serif, dark grey #545454, smaller. Chart label and axis labels in MessinaSans sans-serif, dark grey #545454; chart title in SemiBold. Footer text in light grey #A6A6A6 with the author name in Bold and date and page number in Book weight. Accent colour is red #E8453C for Nvidia; Intel is dark grey #545454. Title size about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, body and axis labels about 1.9%. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey. No shadows, gradients or corner radii.

Values are unlabelled; approximate readings: OpenAI points about 1,190 (Dec 2022), about 1,165 (Jun 2023), about 1,250 (Nov 2023), about 1,105 (Dec 2023), about 1,255 (Feb 2024), about 1,285 (Jun 2024), about 1,335 and 1,300 (Sep 2024), about 1,365 (Dec 2024), about 1,330 to 1,345 (Feb 2025), about 1,345 to 1,410 (Mar to May 2025); the highest grey points reach about 1,445 and 1,460 near May 2025, and the lowest grey points sit near 810 to 850 around mid 2023. Legend at left: a red dot labelled OpenAI above a grey dot labelled All others. No highlighted points beyond the red series colour.

Source line sits bottom-left at x 20.5% in the lower grey band; the footer (author, date, slide number) is right-aligned at the bottom right, ending at about x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

One scatter chart with a connected line series, titled 'Model quality (LMArena) by release date'. Y axis: model quality score (LMArena rating, no unit printed), range 750 to 1,500, labelled ticks at 750, 1,000, 1,250 and 1,500, with dotted light-grey horizontal gridlines at 1,000, 1,250 and 1,500 and a solid grey baseline at 750. X axis: release date, Dec 2022 to Jun 2025, labelled at Dec 2022, Jun 2023, Dec 2023, Jun 2024, Dec 2024 and Jun 2025, with about six months per label interval. Series: OpenAI, red (#E8473B) filled circles, about 25 points, several highlighted in the Dec 2024 to Apr 2025 cluster; DeepSeek, dark charcoal (#3F3F3F) filled circles joined by a thick dark charcoal line, about 12 points rising from about Aug 2024 at about 1,077 to about Dec 2024 near 1,300 and then flattening near 1,370 by about Apr 2025 (estimates; no values printed); All others, mid grey (#A6A6A6) filled circles with thin white outlines, about 110 points spanning roughly 800 to 1,450 with the densest group between 1,000 and 1,250. Legend at left: red dot OpenAI, dark dot joined by a line DeepSeek, grey dot All others. Highlighted points: the DeepSeek series is emphasised by its darker colour and connecting line; the most recent points (about Apr 2025, about 1,370 to 1,440) are the top right of the cluster. A placed raster image (421 by 250 pixels) sits over the upper right of the plot area at about x 69.2%, y 40.9%, width 21.9%, height 23.1%, with its content matching the scatter points in that region. Nothing is cropped or boxed, and there is no photograph or logo.

**Design**

Background #F1F1F1 light grey with a white band #FFFFFF across the middle. Title in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif with regular weight, colour #545454 (dark grey), about 5.56% of slide height. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, about 2.59% of slide height. Chart title and legend labels in MessinaSans-SemiBold and MessinaSans-Regular sans-serif, #535353 to #545554, about 1.85% to 2.22% of slide height. Source line in MessinaSans-Regular, #545454. Footer in MessinaSans-Bold for the name (#A6A6A6) and MessinaSans-Book for the date and page number (#A6A6A6). Accent colours: OpenAI red #E8473B, DeepSeek charcoal #3F3F3F, others grey #A6A6A6. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey (#BFBFBF). Baseline is a solid thin grey line (#8C8C8C). Shapes are flat circles with no shadows or gradients.

### Slide 18

**Text**

Ongoing price collapse / “An LLM is the most expensive and fastest-depreciating asset in tech history" / Price per MTOK ($) for model equivalence (log scale) / 100.0 / ChatGPT 4 / ChatGPT 4o / 10.0 / 1.0 / 0.1 / 0.0 / Dec 2022 / Jun 2023 / Dec 2023 / Jun 2024 / Dec 2024 / Source: Stanford AI Index / Benedict Evans / –– May 2025 / 18

**Layout**

Two horizontal bands frame the slide: a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5% of height) holding the title and subtitle, and a light grey footer band (bottom 86.1 to 100%) holding the source line, author, date and page number. Between them a white content panel spans the full width from 20.5% to 86.1%; the chart fills the panel from about x 6% to x 96.6%. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.5%, as are the chart subtitle and the source line, so all text shares one left margin; the author, date and page number are right-aligned near x 96.5% on the footer baseline. The page number sits bottom right at x 94.5%, y 91.1%.

**Visuals**

One line chart (log scale), drawn as a placed image occupying x 29.5% to 96.5%, y 34.3% to 82.4%. Title printed above plot: "Price per MTOK ($) for model equivalence (log scale)". Y axis: logarithmic, labelled 0.0 (baseline at bottom), 0.1, 1.0, 10.0, 100.0 (top gridline); units are dollars per million tokens (MTOK). X axis: time, labelled Dec 2022, Jun 2023, Dec 2023, Jun 2024, Dec 2024, with the plotted range running from roughly Oct 2022 to Dec 2024. Legend at left (x about 6%, y 37% to 40%): dark grey dot-and-line marker labelled ChatGPT 4; red dot-and-line marker labelled ChatGPT 4o. Series ChatGPT 4 (dark grey line, smoothed, with circular markers; values are not printed, so these are estimates): about 37 at about Oct 2022; about 3.2 at about Jun 2023; about 0.72 at about Oct 2023; about 0.5 at about Jan 2024; about 0.26 at about Jun 2024; about 0.13 at about Jul 2024; about 0.10 at about Aug 2024 (end). Series ChatGPT 4o (red line, smoothed, red circular markers; values not printed, estimates): about 7.5 at about Jun 2024 minus a little, placed near Apr to May 2024 start; about 6 at about Jun 2024; about 4.5 at about Aug 2024; about 2.2 at about Oct 2024; about 0.12 at about Nov 2024; about 0.06 at about Dec 2024 (end). No points are highlighted differently; all markers are the same size. Gridlines are light dotted horizontal lines at each labelled decade; the x axis is a thin solid grey baseline at 0.0. The second placed image (x 71.7% to 96.6%, y 34.4% to 82.4%) overlaps the right portion of the chart area and carries no separate content beyond the chart itself.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 across the full slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel covering the middle band. Restrained, minimal, grey-on-grey look. Title in a high-contrast serif (Messina Modern style, regular weight), dark grey, roughly 5.6% of slide height (about 6% cap height for the capital O and cap height about 0.7 of the size). Subtitle quotation in the same serif, regular weight, about 2.6% of height. Chart title, legend and axis labels in a humanist grotesque sans (Messina Sans family), book/regular weight, about 1.9% of height; chart title slightly larger and semibold at about 2.2%. Footer author name bold, date and page number book weight, all light grey. Colours: background band #F1F1F1, panel #FFFFFF, title and subtitle #545454, body #535353 to #545554, ChatGPT 4 series dark grey about #545454, ChatGPT 4o series red about #E8473B, footer text #A6A6A6. Lines are thin (about 1 px at this resolution) for gridlines and the baseline; series lines are about 3 px, smoothed curves; markers are filled circles about 2% of slide width in diameter. No shadows, gradients, corner radii or borders.

### Slide 19

**Text**

“The Jevons Paradox!” / Economics for beginners: lower prices generally mean more use, but not necessarily more revenue or profits / Global mobile industry since 2010 (indexed, log scale) / Mobile data traffic / Telecoms stocks / 1,000,000 / 100,000 / 10,000 / 1,000 / 100 / 10 / 1 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / 2024 / Source: Ericsson, MSCI World Telecommunications index / Benedict Evans / –– May 2025 / 19

**Layout**

Full-bleed slide split into a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5%) and a light grey footer band (below 86.1%) with a white content panel between them. The title starts at x 20.5% and the subtitle, chart title, y-axis labels and source line share a left text edge near x 20.5%, with the legend pushed out to the left margin at x 4.8%. The footer carries the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right, with the page number ending near x 95.5%. The chart spans the full width of the white panel, and the x-axis year labels sit beneath the baseline.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Type: line chart with two series on a logarithmic y-axis. Title: 'Global mobile industry since 2010 (indexed, log scale)'. Y-axis: logarithmic, indexed value, ticks labelled 1, 10, 100, 1,000, 10,000, 100,000 and 1,000,000, with a dotted light grey gridline at each tick; range 1 to 1,000,000 (1 at the bottom baseline, 1,000,000 at the top gridline). X-axis: years from 2010 to 2024, labelled 2010, 2015, 2020 and 2024, one data point per year, with a solid mid-grey baseline at value 1. Series 1, 'Mobile data traffic', red line (about #E8453C), about 2.5 pt thick: 2010 = 100 (indexed start), 2011 about 250, 2012 about 650, 2013 about 1,400, 2014 about 2,700, 2015 about 4,000, 2016 about 6,000, 2017 about 11,000, 2018 about 22,000, 2019 about 34,000, 2020 about 50,000, 2021 about 68,000, 2022 about 95,000, 2023 about 120,000, 2024 about 150,000 (values estimated from the plotted line; no values printed). Series 2, 'Telecoms stocks', dark grey line (about #4A4A4A), about 2.5 pt thick: 2010 = 100 (indexed start), 2011 about 95, 2012 about 100, 2013 about 115, 2014 about 115, 2015 about 110, 2016 about 112, 2017 about 118, 2018 about 110, 2019 about 130, 2020 about 160, 2021 about 170, 2022 about 125, 2023 about 170, 2024 about 240 (estimated). Legend: top-left of chart, two entries with short line keys, red key for Mobile data traffic and dark grey key for Telecoms stocks; no highlighted points or annotations.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands framing a pure white (#FFFFFF) content panel. Title in a high-contrast serif (Messina Modern Regular, a transitional-style serif), mid grey #545454, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height with cap height about 3.9%. Subtitle in the same serif, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and all chart labels, legend and footer text in a geometric grotesque sans-serif (Messina Sans): chart title SemiBold #545554 at about 2.2%, axis and legend labels Regular #535353 or #545554 at about 1.85%, source line Regular #545454 at 1.85%. Footer author name Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number Book #A6A6A6 at 1.85%. Accent colour is the red series line (about #E8453C). Gridlines are thin dotted light grey lines. No shadows, gradients, rounded corners or boxes; the chart uses only hairlines and two stroked series lines.

**Layout**

Two-band layout on a light grey field: a grey header band holds the left-aligned serif title and subtitle, a white panel carries the chart, and a grey footer band holds the source lines at left and the author, date and page number at right. The text column starts at about 20.5% of slide width, with the legend sitting at the far left margin around 4.8%. The title sits top-left at about 5.6% of slide height, the page number sits bottom-right at about 94.3%, and there is no separate logo.

**Visuals**

One scatter chart. Type: scatter plot. Title printed above the plot: 'Model quality (LMArena) versus price per million output tokens (USD), March 2025'. X axis: 'price per million output tokens (USD)' with tick labels $0, $25, $50, $75, $100, $125, $150 (linear, range about $0 to $150, grey tick labels, no axis title printed). Y axis: model quality score with tick labels 1,100, 1,200, 1,300, 1,400, 1,500 (range about 1,100 to 1,530, no axis title printed). Horizontal dotted light grey gridlines at 1,200, 1,300, 1,400 and 1,500; solid thin grey baseline at 1,100 along the x axis. Legend at left: red dot OpenAI, dark grey dot DeepSeek, mid grey dot Others*. Series values are not printed, so all values are estimates marked about. OpenAI (red, #E8403A-like, about 5 points): about $10 at about 1,365; about $4.5 at about 1,304; about $0.7 at about 1,272; about $60 at about 1,352; about $150 at about 1,411 (the rightmost point, the highest-priced model). DeepSeek (dark grey #3A3A3A, 2 points): about $2 at about 1,362; about $1 at about 1,318. Others* (grey #AAAAAA, about 30 points, clustered): about $10 at about 1,440; about $0.5 at about 1,357; about $6 at about 1,336; about $10 at about 1,302; about $15 at about 1,308; about $15 at about 1,283; about $3.5 at about 1,267; about $3 at about 1,246; about $4 at about 1,236; about $8 at about 1,252; about $1 at about 1,282; about $1 at about 1,257; about $0.5 at about 1,248; about $0 at about 1,307; about $0.5 at about 1,218 and 1,212; about $0 at about 1,197, 1,190 and 1,177. No points are highlighted beyond their colour; markers are filled circles about 1.5% of slide width with thin white outlines. Points overlap heavily near $0 to $2 and 1,170 to 1,310.

**Layout**

Full-width slide split into three horizontal bands: a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5%) holding the title and subtitle, a white middle band (20.5% to 86.1%) holding the chart, and a light grey footer band (86.1% to 100%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. **Visuals**

Chart: scatter plot with a fitted red curve, titled 'Model quality (LMArena) versus price per million output tokens (USD), March 2025'. X axis: price per million output tokens in USD, range $0 to $150, labelled ticks at $0, $25, $50, $75, $100, $125, $150; no axis title printed. Y axis: model quality score (LMArena rating, unitless), range 1,100 to 1,500, labelled ticks at 1,100, 1,200, 1,300, 1,400, 1,500; horizontal dotted gridlines at each 100-point tick; solid thin grey baseline at 1,100 along the x axis. ### Slide 22

**Text**

“Feeds & speeds” and a lot of acronyms
Hundred of models, dozens of benchmarks and acronyms - MTOK replaces MHz
PC Magazine Network Edition December 7 1993 cover
Maximum PC January 2002 cover
PC Magazine October 21 1997 cover
Benedict Evans
–– May 2025
22

**Layout**

Three magazine covers overlap in a horizontal row across the centre of the slide, with the left cover at the left, the Maximum PC cover in front in the centre, and the right cover at the right. The title sits top-left at about 5.6% from the top, with a subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned to a left margin of about 20.5%. The footer line sits at the bottom right at about 91% from the top: author name in bold, an en-dash and date, then the page number at the far right.

**Visuals**

Three photographs of magazine covers, each a scanned front cover in portrait orientation, shown whole and not cropped, each with a soft drop shadow. Left cover: PC Magazine 'Network Edition', dated December 7 1993, Volume 12 Number 21, $2.95. It has a red PC masthead, a printer photo, 'FIRST LOOKS: Get Stunning Color Output From Canon's N... $719 Ink Jet', a blue-tinted 'Blazing Video!' item, a 'Network Management' item, and a large blue '486 BUYERS' GUIDE' cover line with the subhead 'PC Labs Tests 191 of the Fastest PCs Money Can Buy'. A yellow 'THE' badge and a red-and-yellow 'NETWORK EDITION' sticker sit on the masthead; a column of beige '486' desktop PC photos runs down the right edge, partly hidden by the centre cover. Centre cover: Maximum PC, January 2002, yellow background with white sunburst rays, a black 'MAXIMUM PC' masthead, the white headline 'THE SPEED ISSUE', a grey beige desktop tower PC photo at the centre-bottom, a barcode at bottom right and the text 'Quick, Quicker & Quickest - the test results have spoken!'. Right cover: PC Magazine, October 21 1997, Vol. 16 No. 18, white background with a red PC masthead, blue 'Reduce Your PC Expenses' headline, a large black '56K' with red 'MODEMS', an orange and blue modem photograph with motion blur and the label 'SupraExpress 56'. The composition has the covers arranged in a fanned, overlapping row with the centre cover fully visible and the side covers partly hidden. Lighting is flat and even, as on a scan. Dominant colours: yellow, blue, red, black, white, orange.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 fill covering the whole slide. Plain light-grey background with no gradient or pattern. Title in a grey serif (MessinaModern-Regular), about #545454, large. Subtitle in the same serif, about #545454, smaller. Footer in grey sans-serif, about #A6A6A6: the author name bold (MessinaSans-Bold), date and page number in MessinaSans-Book. Covers have soft drop shadows and no borders or corner radius. No accent colours in the layout apart from the magazine covers themselves.

### Slide 23

**Text**

Product, brand or distribution? / The models may be commodities, but ChatGPT is the only ‘AI’ brand with consumer awareness / Apple app store ranking, USA / ChatGPT / CoPilot / Gemini / Perplexity / Claude / 1 / 50 / 100 / 150 / 200 / 250 / Jun 2023 / Dec 2023 / Jun 2024 / Dec 2024 / May 2025 / Source: SimilarWeb / Benedict Evans –– May 2025 / 23

**Layout**

Full-bleed three-band layout: a light grey header band holds the title and subtitle, a white middle band holds the chart, and a light grey footer band holds the source line at left and the author, date and slide number at right. Title and subtitle start at the same left edge (about 20.5% of width), with the chart title and the legend at the left of the chart area. The chart plot area spans about 20.5% to 95.4% of width and 36% to 80% of height. The slide number sits at the far right of the footer, about 94% across.

**Visuals**

One line chart, titled 'Apple app store ranking, USA'. Type: line chart with an inverted vertical axis. Y axis: app store rank, 1 at top to 250 at bottom, with labelled ticks at 1, 50, 100, 150, 200 and 250, and light dotted horizontal gridlines at each tick. Note: the measured text for the tick labels reads '-50', '-100', '-150', '-200' and '-250', but the rendered slide shows positive values 50 to 250. X axis: date, from Jun 2023 to May 2025, with labelled ticks at Jun 2023, Dec 2023, Jun 2024, Dec 2024 and May 2025; a thin grey baseline sits at the bottom of the plot at rank 250. Series, all with no printed data values: ChatGPT, solid red line (about #E8473B) hovering near rank 1 across the whole period, dipping to about rank 20 to 45 in late 2023 and briefly in early 2025. CoPilot, solid black line, spiking in sharp drops to about rank 135 to 200 at several points between late 2023 and mid 2024, then many deep drops to about rank 110 to 210 in April and May 2025. Gemini, solid mid-grey line (about #AAAAAA), first appearing at about rank 20 in Dec 2024, falling to about rank 60 to 135 in early 2025 and ending near rank 60 in May 2025. Perplexity, sparse dark grey dots, appearing around rank 110 to 150 in early 2025 and in a dense cluster of drops to about rank 200 in late April and May 2025. Claude, pale grey line (about #D9D9D9), a short segment near rank 60 to 145 around Jun 2024. Legend at left, top to bottom: ChatGPT (red line swatch), CoPilot (black line swatch), Gemini (grey line swatch), Perplexity (small dot), Claude (pale grey line swatch). No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86% of height. Clean, minimal editorial look. Header and footer bands are flat light grey #F1F1F1; the chart band is flat white #FFFFFF. Title is a high-contrast serif in grey #545454, subtitle is a serif in grey #545454, and chart and body labels are a neo-grotesque sans-serif in grey #535353 to #545454. The footer author name is bold sans-serif in light grey #A6A6A6, and the date and slide number are book-weight sans in #A6A6A6. Title is about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle about 2.6%; chart title about 2.2%; labels, legend and footer about 1.9%. Chart lines are about 2 to 3 px thick with round joins; gridlines are thin dotted grey; the baseline is a thin solid grey line. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 24

**Text**

‘Big Tech’ strategies: “how do we use this?”
Incumbents always try to absorb the new thing into their existing strategies
Build a hardware platform
Nvidia
Make it a commodity
Amazon, Meta
Sell the APIs
Amazon, Google, Microsoft
Make it a feature
Google, Meta, Microsoft, Apple
Benedict Evans –– May 2025 24

**Layout**

Four white circles sit in a single horizontal row across the middle-lower part of the slide, evenly spaced with equal widths, each with its label stacked and centred inside. The title is centred horizontally near the top, with a smaller subtitle centred beneath it, and a footer line of author, date and page number sits at bottom right with a right margin of about 5%.

**Visuals**

Four circles (outline-stroked ellipses with white fill), no photographs or charts. Circle 1 (left) has a red stroke, about #E84C3D, approximately 0.3% of slide width thick; circles 2, 3 and 4 have dark grey strokes about #545454 of the same thickness. Each circle is about 21.7% of slide width in diameter, centred vertically around y 53%. Circle 1 at x 4.0 to 25.7; circle 2 at x 27.5 to 49.2; circle 3 at x 51.0 to 72.7; circle 4 at x 74.5 to 96.2. Circle 1 contains 'Build a / hardware / platform' bold with 'Nvidia' beneath; circle 2 contains 'Make it a / commodity' bold with 'Amazon, Meta' beneath; circle 3 contains 'Sell the APIs' bold with 'Amazon, / Google, / Microsoft' beneath; circle 4 contains 'Make it a / feature' bold with 'Google, Meta, / Microsoft, Apple' beneath. No shadows or gradients.

**Design**

Background: Flat solid #F1F1F1 light grey. Flat light grey background #F1F1F1. Title and body text in dark grey #545454 serif (MessinaModern, Regular for title and subtitle, SemiBold for circle headings, Regular for circle body). Title is large, about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle headings about 3.3%, circle body about 3.0%. Footer in sans-serif (MessinaSans) in light grey #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height; author name Bold, rest Book. Only accent is the red outline on the first circle. Circles have no corner radii since they are ellipses; stroke is solid, no shadows.

### Slide 25

**Text**

But what does this mean for the rest of us?
How many acronyms do you need to care about if you’re not an Nvidia shareholder?
Massive / investment and / FOMO in tech
Models getting / better and / cheaper
Models are / commodities, / for now
Benedict Evans –– May 2025 25

**Layout**

Three equal circles sit in a horizontal row across the centre-right of the slide, with the title and subtitle left-aligned at the top and a footer line at bottom right. The title block starts at about 20.5% from the left and the circles span roughly 19.5% to 95.8% of the width; the footer runs right to about 95.5% with the slide number at the right edge.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles of equal size in a row, each about 22.1% of slide width and 39.3% of slide height. Left circle dark grey #535353 with white serif text 'Massive / investment and / FOMO in tech'. Middle circle dark grey #535353 with white serif text 'Models getting / better and / cheaper'. Right circle red #EB483F with white serif text 'Models are / commodities, / for now'. No photographs, charts or icons; the circles are flat with no outline or shadow.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Plain light grey background #F1F1F1 with no texture. Title and subtitle in grey #545454 in a Messina Modern serif (Regular); title about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%. Circle labels in white #FFFFFF Messina Modern Book serif at about 3.3% of slide height. Footer in Messina Sans, Bold for the name and Book for the date and number, at about 1.85% in #A6A6A6. Accent red #EB483F used on the right circle only. Shapes are flat with no corner radius, shadow or gradient.

**Layout**

A full-width light grey header band holds the title at left x 20.5% and the subtitle beneath it, both left-aligned to the same margin. Below it a white chart panel spans the middle with the chart title at x 20.6% and a legend at far left (x 6.2%). The bar chart fills the panel from x 17.5% to 95.4%, with a light grey footer band holding the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and slide number at right, the slide number at x 94.5%.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked vertical bar chart. Title: How many people use generative AI chatbots in the USA? Y axis: percentage of people, 0% to 40%, gridlines at 10% intervals (dotted light grey lines at 10, 20, 30, 40%), no unit label beyond the % sign. X axis: six survey sources as categories, labels: Pew, Aug 2024 / CCIA, Nov 2024 / NBER RPS, Nov 2024 / NBER SWAA, Dec 2024 / Bain, Dec 2024 / KPMG, Dec 2024. Series 1 (bottom, red): Daily active, #EB483F. Series 2 (top, dark grey): Weekly active, #535353. Values are not printed on the slide; estimates: Pew daily about 11%, weekly about 26% (total); CCIA daily about 13%, weekly about 29% (total); NBER RPS daily about 9%, weekly about 32% (total); NBER SWAA daily about 7%, weekly about 34% (total); Bain daily about 12%, weekly about 31% (total); KPMG daily about 12%, weekly about 24% (total). Legend at upper left of chart: dark grey square labelled Weekly active above red square labelled Daily active. Bars have thin white separator lines between the two segments. No highlighted points. Dotted gridlines run horizontally across the plot; the 0% baseline is a solid thin grey line.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands framing a white #FFFFFF chart panel. Title in MessinaModern-Regular (a modern serif, regular weight) in #545454, large, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of slide height, #545454. Chart title and axis labels in MessinaSans (sans-serif) about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height; chart title SemiBold #545454. Accent red #EB483F for daily series; dark grey #535353 for weekly series. Footer author name in bold #A6A6A6, date and slide number in book weight #A6A6A6. Flat style: no shadows, no gradients, square bar corners, hairline dotted gridlines.

### Slide 32

**Text**

Corporate hesitation / For enterprises, use cases are easier to see but implementation is hard / Enterprise reasons to delay LLM deployment, top three choices (December 2024) / 50% / 40% / 30% / 20% / 10% / 0% / Security & privacy / Expertise / Error rates / ROI unclear / Data not ready Intellectual property / rights / Regulation / Use cases / Source: Bain / Benedict Evans –– May 2025 / 32

**Layout**

Single content slide with a full-width grey header band holding a left-aligned title and subtitle starting at about x 20.5%, a white body panel spanning the full width from y 20.5% to 86.1% containing a single bar chart whose plot area runs from about x 17.5% to 95.4%, and a grey footer band below with the source line at left and the author, date and slide number at right. Left margin of text is about 20.5%; the slide number sits at about x 94.3% and the right edge of the chart is about 95.4%.

**Visuals**

Chart: vertical bar chart, untitled axis labels, with the chart title 'Enterprise reasons to delay LLM deployment, top three choices (December 2024)'. Y-axis: percentage of respondents, 0% to 50%, gridlines every 10%, dotted light grey (#B0B0B0, about 0.5 pt). X-axis: categories, no unit, baseline solid grey line. Seven bars? No: eight bars, all with about values because no values are printed. Security & privacy: about 44%, dark grey #535353. Expertise: about 42%, #535353. Error rates: about 39%, #535353. ROI unclear: about 29%, red #EB483F, highlighted. Data not ready: about 27%, #535353. Intellectual property rights: about 27%, #535353. Regulation: about 25%, #535353. Use cases: about 18%, red #EB483F, highlighted. Legend: none. Highlighted points: the ROI unclear and Use cases bars are red. Bars are rectangles with square corners, width about 3.8% of slide width, evenly spaced.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, with a full-width white #FFFFFF body panel between them. Title colour grey #545454 in a MessinaModern serif regular, about 5.6% of slide height, with the subtitle in the same serif at about 2.6%. Chart title in MessinaSans semibold #545554 at about 2.2%. Axis labels in MessinaSans regular #545554 at about 1.85%. Bars dark grey #535353 with red #EB483F accents. Footer text in MessinaSans, source line #545454 regular; author name bold #A6A6A6 and date book #A6A6A6, all about 1.85%. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 33

**Text**

The future always takes time / Cloud is old and boring - but still only 30% of workflows / Enterprise workloads in public cloud / Expected in 3 years / Today / 60% / 50% / 40% / 30% / 20% / 10% / 0% / Dec 2014 / Dec 2015 / Dec 2016 / Dec 2017 / Dec 2018 / Dec 2019 / Dec 2020 / Dec 2021 / Mar 2023 / Jun 2024 / Source: Goldman Sachs CIO Survey / Benedict Evans / –– May 2025 / 33

**Layout**

Full-width header band on a light grey field: the title sits top-left at about 20.5% from the left edge and 5.6% from the top, with the subtitle beneath it, both left-aligned to the same x-position. A white content panel spans the full width from 20.5% to 86.1% of the slide height; inside it a chart title sits at x 20.6%, y 27.4%, a legend at the left margin (x 4.8%), and a line chart fills the plot area from about x 20.5% to 95.4% and y 36% to 80%. A footer row below the white panel holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and slide number at right, with the slide number right-aligned at about x 94.3% to 95.5%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Enterprise workloads in public cloud', with no axis titles. Y-axis: percentage, 0% to 60% in 10-point steps, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each step and a solid grey baseline at 0%. X-axis: time from Dec 2014 to about Sep 2024 on a roughly uniform time scale, labelled Dec 2014, Dec 2015, Dec 2016, Dec 2017, Dec 2018, Dec 2019, Dec 2020, Dec 2021, Mar 2023 and Jun 2024. Two smoothed curved series. Series 1, 'Expected in 3 years', dark grey line (#4F4F4F) about 2.5 pt thick: about 9% at Dec 2014, about 14% mid-2015, about 16% at Dec 2015, about 19% mid-2016, about 38% at Dec 2016, about 33% mid-2017, about 35% at Dec 2017, about 34% mid-2018, about 38% at Dec 2018, about 36% mid-2019, about 43% at Dec 2019, about 42% in 2020, about 40% at Dec 2020, about 42% at Dec 2021, about 51% at Mar 2023, about 47% late 2023, about 47.5% in early 2024, about 46% at Jun 2024. Series 2, 'Today', red line (#E8473F) about 2.5 pt thick: about 4% at Dec 2014, about 6% at Dec 2015, about 18% at Dec 2016, about 20% mid-2017, about 19% at Dec 2017, about 18% mid-2018, about 26% at Dec 2018, about 19% mid-2019, about 23% at Dec 2019, about 22% in 2020, about 21% at Dec 2020, about 22% at Dec 2021, about 31% at Mar 2023, about 29% late 2023, about 29.5% in early 2024, about 28% at Jun 2024. Legend at upper left of the chart: a short dark grey dash followed by 'Expected in 3 years', and a short red dash followed by 'Today'. No highlighted points or annotations. Data points are not printed on the slide, so all values are estimates.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from 20.5% to 86.1% of the height. Light grey background (#F1F1F1) for the top header band and the bottom footer band, with a white panel (#FFFFFF) between them. Title in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif with regular weight, size about 5.6% of slide height, dark grey #545454. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular at about 2.6% of slide height, #545454. Chart title and axis labels in MessinaSans, a sans-serif; chart title SemiBold at about 2.2%, axis labels Regular at about 1.85%, colour #545454. Footer: source line in MessinaSans Regular #545454; author name in MessinaSans Bold #A6A6A6; date in MessinaSans Book #A6A6A6; slide number in MessinaSans Book #A6A6A6. Accent colours are the dark grey (#4F4F4F) and red (#E8473F) chart lines. Lines are flat with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners. Gridlines are light grey dotted hairlines.

### Slide 34

**Text**

It’s always different
Every platform shift looks like fundamental discontinuity
“What’s a use-case for 3G?”
Smartphones replace
PCs at 5-10x the scale
“Why would anyone trust
Amazon with their data?”
All new software is
built on the cloud
“LLMs are slow, expensive
and unpredictable”
True, but…
Benedict Evans
–– May 2025
34

**Layout**

Single-column rows on a light grey field: a left-aligned title and subtitle sit in the top band starting at about x 20.5%, and three horizontal white bands stack below, each holding a left quote, a red dotted arrow, and a right-hand statement in two columns starting at about x 20.5% and x 73.3%. A grey footer strip at the bottom carries the author, date and slide number right-aligned at about x 75% to 96%.

**Visuals**

Three identical red dotted arrows, each a horizontal dotted line with a solid filled triangular arrowhead at its right end, running from about x 49.4% to 65% of the slide width. Background light grey #F1F1F1 with full-width white #FFFFFF bands for the three content rows, separated by thin #F2F2F2 gaps. Body statements in MessinaModern Book serif, grey #545454, about 3.3% of slide height. The last statement 'True, but…' is in MessinaModern BookItalic. Footer in MessinaSans, grey #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height, with 'Benedict Evans' in Bold. Arrows are red #E94B3C dotted lines with filled triangular heads. No shadows, gradients, corner radii or rounded shapes.

### Slide 35

**Text**

What’s the standard pattern?
How do we always deploy new technologies?
Absorb
Make it a feature
Automate obvious
use-cases
Innovate
New products,
bundling and
unbundling
Disrupt
Redefine the
question
Benedict Evans
–– May 2025
35

**Layout**

Three equal white circles sit in a single horizontal row across the middle of the slide, spaced evenly with grey arrow chevrons between them, and the row is right of a wide empty left margin. The title sits top-left at about x 20.5% and the subtitle sits directly beneath it, both left-aligned on a shared edge. The footer line sits bottom-right with the author name, date and slide number on one baseline, and the slide number at the far right edge near x 94%.

**Visuals**

Three white filled circles of equal size (each about 21.7% of slide width by 38.5% of slide height) with thin dark grey outlines (#545454, about 2 px). The first and second circles have dark grey outlines; the third circle has a red outline (#E94B3C-like, about 2 px). Between circles are two solid grey right-pointing triangles (#A6A6A6), one between circle 1 and 2 at about x 43.2% to 45.1%, y 49.8% to 56.6%, and one between circle 2 and 3 at about x 70.7% to 72.6%, same vertical position. Circle contents: 'Absorb' (bold heading), 'Make it a feature', 'Automate obvious use-cases'; 'Innovate' (bold heading), 'New products, bundling and unbundling'; 'Disrupt' (bold heading), 'Redefine the question'. No photographs, charts or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Flat light grey background (#F1F1F1) with no gradient or texture. Title and body text in dark grey #545454; footer author name in lighter grey #A6A6A6 bold, date and slide number in #A6A6A6 regular. Accent colour is red (#E94B3C) used only on the third circle outline; grey #A6A6A6 for arrows. Typeface is a transitional serif (Messina Modern) in Regular, Book and SemiBold weights; footer uses Messina Sans in Bold and Book. **Layout**

Two-column split: a light grey text column fills the left half and a black-and-white photograph fills the right half, running full height to the right edge. **Visuals**

One photograph, a black-and-white product photo of an Apple II personal computer, placed in the right half at x 46.8%, y 0%, w 53.2%, h 100%, full-bleed to the top, bottom and right edges and cropped at the right edge (a Disk II floppy drive unit is cut off by the frame). Subject: an Apple II beige case with a keyboard in the foreground, the Apple logo badge on the front of the keyboard base, and a CRT monitor at the rear. The monitor screen shows a VisiCalc spreadsheet in monospaced text with a title line 'FINANCIAL HIGHLIGHTS', 'APPLE COMPUTER, INC', 'FOR THE FISCAL YEARS ENDING SEPTEMBER', 'DOLLARS AND SHARES IN THOUSANDS', column headers '1982' and '1983', and rows including NET SALES, NET INCOME, EARNINGS PER SHARE, PRE-TAX RETURN ON NET SALE, and CASH AND CASH EQUIVALENTS / ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE with numeric values. Composition: three-quarter view from the front-left, the computer occupying most of the frame, dark grey gradient background darkening toward the top-left and top-right corners. Lighting: soft, directional light from the upper left, producing a shadow beneath the keyboard. Dominant colours: greys and off-white, with a black screen. No overlay or tint.

Title is about 5.6% of slide height; body quote and paragraph are about 3.3% of slide height; footer and source are about 1.9%. Line spacing for body is about 4% of slide height between baselines. Join dozens of agile telecom companies that use Symphonica to create, test and launch new TM Forum-certified automation workflows and network connectors in minutes. / TALK WITH AN EXPERT / Launch New Services Immediately with Symphonica OSS / Benedict Evans / –– May 2025 / 41

**Layout**

Full-width title block in the upper left with the title at top and subtitle beneath it, both left-aligned at about x 20.5 percent. Three browser-window screenshots sit in a staggered horizontal row across the middle of the slide, overlapping: bloop.ai at left (back), symphonica.com at right (back), and the Y Combinator directory window in the centre (front, lowest). The footer line sits at the bottom right with author name, date and page number on one baseline at about y 91 percent. No logo in the corners and no visible margin rules.

**Visuals**

Three macOS-style browser window mockups with soft drop shadows and rounded window corners, each with red, yellow and green traffic-light dots at top left and a URL bar. Left window (bloop.ai, back, x 18.4 percent, y 22.5 percent, w 29.4 percent, h 60.4 percent): dark near-black navy page #10131C with white bold bloop. wordmark at top left and hamburger icon at top right; large centred bold grey sans-serif headline in light grey #C8CCD4 fading to darker grey at the edges, five lines of text; smaller grey subtitle in #9AA0AA beneath; the headline is clipped at the right edge of the window and continues under the centre window. Centre window (Y Combinator directory, front, x 42.4 percent, y 27.7 percent, w 29.4 percent, h 60.4 percent): off-white #FBFAF5 list background with thin light grey row dividers; six company rows, each with a round logo at left, bold company name, small location, one-line description, and small grey tag chips. Company logos: Vantel, a navy angular outline mark on white; Fira, a white stacked-bar mark on black circle; assistant-ui, a white chat-bubble mark on black circle; Artifact, a purple letter A mark on near-black circle; Axal, a white fork-arrow mark on black circle; Dex, a white stylised D mark on near-black circle. Each row has a small orange W25 badge. Right window (symphonica.com, back, x 66.2 percent, y 22.5 percent, w 29.4 percent, h 60.4 percent): white navigation bar with hamburger icon, blue Symphonica wordmark, search icon, Start for Free link and outlined LOG IN button in blue; below is a full-width violet-blue #6B5CF0 hero block with centred white text and a white rounded button labelled TALK WITH AN EXPERT in violet text; below that a pale grey-white band with violet headline text. Lighting is flat, screenshots are shown whole, not cropped to a photo frame, and they are unshaded rectangles with no overlay tint.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 fill covering the whole slide. Light neutral grey slide background with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle are in a grey serif (Messina Modern style, a high-contrast transitional serif) in dark grey #545454, regular weight. Footer text is in a sans-serif (Messina Sans style) in light grey #A6A6A6, author name bold and date and page number book weight. Title is about 5.6 percent of slide height, subtitle about 2.6 percent, footer about 1.9 percent. Screenshot windows have thin light grey borders, small corner radii of about 1 percent of slide width, and soft diffuse drop shadows beneath. No accent colour on the slide itself beyond the colours in the screenshots.

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey slide with a white content band in the middle; title and subtitle are left-aligned at about x 20.5% in the top grey header band, the chart fills the white band from about x 17% to 96%, and a grey footer strip holds the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. The slide number 43 sits at the bottom right at about x 94%, y 91%.

**Visuals**

Stacked column chart. Title: 'Enterprise use case adoption rates for generative AI: February, July & December 2024'. Y axis: percent of adoption, range 0% to 70%, gridlines every 10% drawn as light grey dotted lines, labels 0% to 70% at left. X axis: 10 categories with three columns each (February, July, December order from left to right within each group), category labels Software, Customer service, Marketing, Knowledge workers, Operations, IT, Finance, Sales, HR, Legal. Each column is a stack: lower segment Production in red, upper segment Development/Pilot in dark grey #535353, separated by thin white gaps. Values are not printed on the slide, so these are estimates: Software Feb production about 21%, total about 56%; Jul production about 25%, total about 66%; Dec production about 29%, total about 68%. Customer service Feb about 18% / 51%; Jul about 25% / 62%; Dec about 23% / 59%. Marketing Feb about 11% / 49%; Jul about 14% / 53%; Dec about 17% / 55%. Knowledge workers Feb about 10% / 47%; Jul about 16% / 54%; Dec about 19% / 58%. Operations Feb about 8% / 42%; Jul about 9% / 48%; Dec about 15% / 52%. IT Feb about 12% / 40%; Jul about 18% / 48%; Dec about 24% / 60%. Finance Feb about 10% / 37%; Jul about 6% / 24%; Dec about 7% / 35%. Sales Feb about 3% / 23%; Jul about 14% / 48%; Dec about 14% / 55%. HR Feb about 4% / 22%; Jul about 4% / 20%; Dec about 7% / 30%. Legal Feb about 4% / 18%; Jul about 5% / 22%; Dec about 9% / 34%. Legend at upper left of chart: dark grey square with 'Development/Pilot' above red square with 'Production'. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Flat minimal design. Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF for the chart band. Title and subtitle in a serif modern face (MessinaModern-Regular), title colour #545454 and subtitle #545454. Chart title and axis labels in a grotesque sans (MessinaSans family), regular weight, colour #535353 to #545454. Accent colour red for Production segments and dark grey #535353 for Development/Pilot. Title size about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, body and axis labels about 1.9%. Footer text light grey #A6A6A6, author name in bold. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

### Slide 44

**Text**

The future can take time / A quarter of CIOs have launched something - but 40% don’t plan anything until at least 2026 / CIO expected timing for first LLM projects in production, March 2025 / Already deployed / at least one project / H1 2025 / H2 2025 / 2026 / Later or no plan / 0% / 25% / 50% / 75% / 100% / Source: Morgan Stanley CIO Survey / Benedict Evans / –– May 2025 / 44

**Layout**

Two-tone horizontal band layout: a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5% of height) holds the title and subtitle, a white central band (20.5% to 86%) holds a single full-width 100% stacked horizontal bar chart, and a light grey footer band (86% to 100%) holds the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.5% with a left margin of about 20.5%; the chart title sits left-aligned at the same x. The slide number 44 is at right, x 94.3%, in the footer.

**Visuals**

Chart: a single 100% horizontal stacked bar, drawn as one bar of five segments. Chart title: CIO expected timing for first LLM projects in production, March 2025. Horizontal axis runs 0% to 100% with labelled ticks at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75% and 100%, shown as light grey dotted vertical gridlines; no axis unit beyond percentage of CIOs. Vertical axis line at 0% is a thin solid grey line. Segments left to right with their labels and approximate widths read from the axis: Already deployed at least one project, red segment about 0% to 26% (labelled with no printed value); H1 2025, pale pink segment about 26% to 36%; H2 2025, black segment about 36% to 61%; 2026, dark grey segment about 61% to 71%; Later or no plan, mid grey segment about 71% to 100%. The segments have thin white separators. No legend; segment labels sit inside each segment. No values are printed on the segments, so widths are estimates from the axis. Highlighted point: none. Subtitle states 40% do not plan until at least 2026 and a quarter have launched something.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a central white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86% of height. Flat, minimal consulting-style look. Header and footer bands are light grey #F1F1F1 and the chart band is white #FFFFFF. Title is set in a serif (Messina Modern-style, a book serif) in dark grey #545454, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle is the same serif in dark grey #545454 at about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title, axis labels, source line and body labels are a geometric sans (Messina Sans style) at about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height; the chart title is semibold in #545554 and the axis labels are regular in #545554. Footer author name is bold in light grey #A6A6A6 and the date and page number are book weight in #A6A6A6. Bar segment colours: red #EB483F, pale pink #FBDAD9, black #000000, dark grey #535353, mid grey #A7A7A7. Segment labels are white (#FFFFFF) except the pink H1 2025 label which is #535353. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey. Bar segments have square corners, no shadows and no gradients.

### Slide 45

**Text**

Platform shifts mean more tools
SaaS means the typical large enterprise now uses 4-500 apps
Mainframes / One app
PCs / Dozens of apps
Web/Mobile / Hundreds of apps
LLMs / What else can we automate?
Benedict Evans –– May 2025 45

**Layout**

Four circles in a single horizontal row spanning the slide's centre-right, connected by small grey chevron arrows, with the title and subtitle left-aligned at the top and the footer right-aligned along the bottom. The title starts at about 20.5% from the left edge, aligned to the same left margin as the subtitle, and the footer line with author, date and slide number sits at about 91% height on the right.

**Visuals**

Four circles in a horizontal sequence, left to right: (1) dark grey #535353 circle with white text 'Mainframes / One app'; (2) dark grey #535353 circle with white text 'PCs / Dozens of apps'; (3) dark grey #535353 circle with white text 'Web/Mobile / Hundreds of apps'; (4) red #EB483F circle with white text 'LLMs / What else can we automate?'. Between each pair of circles is a small solid light-grey (#A6A6A6) right-pointing triangular chevron, about 2.5% of slide width wide and 6% of slide height tall, pointing right, with no outline. The circles are equal in size (about 16.2% wide by 28.8% tall, so they are ovals rendered as circles), evenly spaced at 20% intervals, with no outlines or shadows. Nothing is photographic; no images, icons or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat background in light grey #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title in a serif face resembling MessinaModern Regular, dark grey #545454, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height for the title size. Subtitle in the same serif face, dark grey #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Circle labels in MessinaModern Book serif, white #FFFFFF, about 3.0% of slide height, with the first line of each circle label set as a heading-like line and the second line as the descriptive line. Footer in MessinaSans, light grey #A6A6A6: 'Benedict Evans' in Bold, followed by '–– May 2025' in Book, and the page number '45' in Book, all about 1.9% of slide height. Accent colour is red #EB483F used only on the fourth circle. Circles are flat fills with no corner radius, shadow or gradient, and chevrons are flat light grey.

### Slide 46

**Text**

Is this a CIO question? CFO? CEO?

Is this an Accenture question? Or a Bain/ BCG/McKinsey question?

Is this top line or bottom line change? Or a disruptive, existential threat?

Benedict Evans –– May 2025 46

**Layout**

A single white rectangular panel sits on a light grey field, inset from the left and right edges and placed in the lower-middle portion of the slide. Three question blocks are left-aligned inside the panel with generous internal padding; there is no title. The footer line sits below the panel, right-aligned to the panel's right edge, with the author name, dash and date, and the slide number at the far right.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

**Layout**

A full-width light grey header band holds the title at top left, with the subtitle beneath it, both aligned to a left margin at about 20.5% of slide width. Below the header, a white content panel fills the middle of the slide, containing two side-by-side line charts (left: New York taxis versus Uber; right: US hotels versus Airbnb), each with a chart heading above and year labels below its horizontal axis. A grey footer strip holds the source line at left and the author, date and slide number at right, with the page number at about 94.4% of slide width at the right margin.

**Visuals**

Two line charts, both smoothed curves with no markers. Left chart: title 'New York taxis versus Uber (trips per day)'. Horizontal axis runs from 2015 to 2024 with labelled ticks at 2015, 2020 and 2024; vertical axis labelled only '0' at the baseline, no units or range printed. Four dotted horizontal gridlines are evenly spaced above the baseline. Series 1, 'Uber etc', in red (about #E8453C): rises from about the baseline-level low in 2015 to a peak around 2019, falls sharply to a trough in 2020, then rises steadily to about the top gridline level by 2024; it ends at the upper right. Series 2, 'Taxis', in dark grey (about #545454): starts high in 2015, declines gradually to a sharp low in 2020, then stays roughly flat to 2024 at a low level; it ends at the lower right. The two lines cross around 2016. Right chart: title 'US hotels versus Airbnb (room revenue)'. Horizontal axis labelled 2015, 2020 and 2024; vertical axis labelled only '0' at the baseline, no units or range printed. Four dotted gridlines. Series 'Hotels' in dark grey (about #545454): starts at a mid-upper level in 2015, rises slightly to a local peak around 2019, drops to a trough in 2020, then rises steeply to about 2023 and ends near the top gridline in 2024. Series 'Airbnb' in red (about #E8453C): begins about 2017 at the baseline, rises gently and roughly steadily to a small dip around 2020, then climbs to a low level by 2024. No values are printed; all positions are estimates. Series end labels are placed at the right ends of the lines. No legend; series are identified by direct labels. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel across the middle band. Clean, minimal editorial style. Header band and footer strip are light grey (#F1F1F1) and the content panel is white (#FFFFFF). Title in a serif face, Messina Modern Regular, dark grey #545454, large (about 5.6% of slide height). Subtitle in the same serif, regular weight, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart headings and series labels are in Messina Sans SemiBold and Regular, #545454 / #535353, about 2.2% and 1.9% of slide height. Source and axis labels are Messina Sans Regular or Book, #545454 / #535353, about 1.9% of slide height. Footer author name is Messina Sans Bold in #A6A6A6; date and page number are Messina Sans Book in #A6A6A6. Lines: chart series about 2 px stroke, grey #545454 and red about #E8453C; gridlines are thin dotted light grey lines; baseline axis lines are thin solid grey. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.
